The auth handshake (the iter4 gate) needed FOUR platform fixes, all constraint #5 (dbus source untouched): (1) NEW registry/native/patches/wasi-libc-overrides/dbus_creds.c linked into all 3 binaries — host_net AF_UNIX has no SCM/ancillary or real SO_PEERCRED and its fds (≥0x40000000) aren't in the WASI fd table, so it provides for host_net fds only: recvmsg() (1-byte credential read), getsockopt(SO_PEERCRED)→uid 0 (single sandbox identity → EXTERNAL auth succeeds), sendmsg() (message writes, send-per-iovec), and __wrap_read (read()→recv(), surfacing would-block as EAGAIN so dbus doesn't treat it as a fatal disconnect). (2) build-dbus.sh: -DSO_PEERCRED=17 -D_GNU_SOURCE so dbus's `#ifdef SO_PEERCRED` credential path (its only EXTERNAL mechanism; wasi hides SO_PEERCRED behind __wasilibc_unmodified_upstream) compiles in; archive dbus_creds.o as libdbuscreds.a (libtool rejects a raw .o in LDFLAGS for libdbus-1.la); -Wl,--wrap=read; force ac_cv_func_poll=yes. (3) RUNTIME net_poll POLLOUT fix (node_import_cache.rs, asset 100→101): POLLOUT was the WRONG 0x002 (that's POLLPRI) — corrected to the real wasi <poll.h> 0x004, keeping 0x002 as a compat bit. dbus forces HAVE_POLL because its select() fallback can't represent fd 0x40000000 in an fd_set, and unlike the X libs it genuinely waits on POLLOUT to flush its auth OK/replies — so the long-latent runtime bug only surfaced now. RESULTS: (1) ★GENERAL PLATFORM FIX — GIO AF_UNIX connect: GLib g_socket_connect writes the native address into a struct sockaddr_storage and g_unix_socket_address_to_native requires destlen>=sizeof(struct sockaddr_un)=110, but wasi's sockaddr_storage was only ~34 bytes (__ss_data[32]) → EVERY GIO AF_UNIX connect failed G_IO_ERROR_NO_SPACE ("Not enough space for socket address"). Fixed __struct_sockaddr_storage.h (all wasm32-wasi* variants) to __ss_data[126] = 128B total (Linux _SS_SIZE); the struct size is baked into compiled code so recompiled glib's gsocket.c/gunixsocketaddress.c/gsocketaddress.c objects + re-archived libgio-2.0.a (via compile_commands.json, NOT meson reconfigure which false-negatives frexp without the cross-env). (3) ★REMAINING BLOCKER: post-auth a GDBus worker thread (client0~thread~child-3) spawns to send the initial Hello method-call + blocks before bytes reach the daemon (daemon stays Authenticated but silent; client returns "The connection is closed"). = GLib GMainContext/GSocket async I/O on a WORKER THREAD over host_net — the cross-thread context wakeup that flushes queued Hello (M8.5 GIO worker-context-wakeup area, pending task #11). 2026-06-24T21b (XU1 iter2 + tooling doc): ROOT CAUSE of the XU1 GDBus worker-thread blocker FOUND (code-confirmed via reading the runner): the host_net socket table (hostNetSockets, node_import_cache.rs ~11144) is a module-level Map that is PER-ISOLATE, and each wasm thread runs in its OWN V8 isolate (~14282, "second-isolate spawn sharing guestSharedMemory" + wasi_thread_start). GDBus auth runs on the MAIN isolate, then GDBus does message I/O on a WORKER thread whose isolate has an EMPTY hostNetSockets → net_send(fd) → getHostNetSocket miss → WASI_ERRNO_BADF → GIO "connection is closed". M8's X clients never hit this (X I/O stays on the GTK main thread); GDBus is the first guest doing socket I/O on a worker thread. FIX DIRECTION: make guest-fd→socketId resolvable across isolates (the real sockets already live process-wide in the sidecar process.unix_sockets keyed by socketId; only the per-isolate JS fd→socketId cache is missing) — resolve fd→socketId via the sidecar, or share the socket registry across thread isolates. Added a debugging tool while diagnosing: SECURE_EXEC_NET_TRACE (per-isolate net_send/recv/poll trace w/ fd+bytes+result; the strace -e network/tcpdump capability) in node_import_cache.rs (asset 101→102), gated + zero-cost off. 2026-06-24T22 (XU1 iter3): fully MAPPED the XU1 GDBus blocker across all 3 layers + wrote the implementation plan (XU1-SOCKET-SHARING-DESIGN.md); deferred the code change behind an M8 regression gate (it touches the shared net.*/readiness machinery the working X path uses — rushing it risks the M8 spine). The blocker: host_net sockets are scoped too narrowly at THREE layers — (1) runner hostNetSockets is a per-isolate Map and each wasm thread is its own V8 isolate, so the GDBus worker isolate can't see the main isolate's socket (net_send→EBADF, NET_TRACE-confirmed: worker 'send fd=...824 BADF known=0'); (2) the sidecar UnixSocket (owned UnixStream + reader thread + event channel) lives in process.unix_sockets keyed by process_id, and a worker thread is a separate active_process ({parent}~thread~{N}, shares parent_kernel_pid but not the socket table); (3) socket_readiness is per-process Arc, signalled into the OWNER. CHOSEN FIX: route a thread's socket op to its owning ancestor process (the sidecar already has descendant_parent_process helpers + the ~thread~ parent-encoded process ids), no connection move needed (only one thread uses the socket post-auth). Part A = runner fd→socketId resolution via a new sidecar net.register_guest_fd/net.resolve_guest_fd registry keyed by (kernel_pid,fd). Part B = sidecar services a thread's net.write/read/poll on the owner's socket+readiness. ★PLUMBING CONSTRAINT found while scoping: service_javascript_sync_rpc gets a single &mut process (NOT the VM/siblings), so Part B needs the caller (which holds the VM) to resolve the owner and pass its socket context in, OR promote socket tables+readiness to per-kernel_pid — a dispatch-plumbing change, not a local edit. That's the concrete reason it's staged with the M8 gate. NEXT: implement Parts A+B, run gdbus-probe (expect PASS) + test-m5-twm (must stay green), then build libxfce4util + xfconf.
2026-06-24T22b (XU1 iter4): ★MAJOR — cross-thread host_net socket sharing IMPLEMENTED + VERIFIED. The GDBus worker thread (its own V8 isolate) now does full socket I/O over the socket the MAIN thread opened: resolves fd->socketId via a new sidecar registry, sends Hello (wrote=128), receives the daemon's replies (got=16/73/153). M8 stays green (test-m5-twm PASS). This was THE root-cause blocker for XU1. Implementation (4 layers): Part A runner (node_import_cache.rs): registerGuestFd on connect/accept, getHostNetSocket resolves a missing host_net fd ([0x40000000,0x50000000), excluding the 0x50000000 kernel-pipe range) via net.resolve_guest_fd + caches, net_set_nonblock propagates, net_close unregisters. Part B sidecar (service.rs): a worker thread's inline net.* data ops dispatch against the owning ancestor process (net_owner_process_id strips ~thread~); net.poll_wait keeps the worker process for its deferred-response channel but waits on the OWNER's socket_readiness (threaded as owner_socket_readiness through both request structs + the net.poll_wait handler); VM-wide guest_net_fds registry on VmState. ★The 4th piece that blocked attempt #1: WASM guest sync-RPCs go through the WASM BRIDGE ALLOWLIST, not service.rs directly — had to register the 3 new methods in wasm.rs switch + v8_runtime.rs map_bridge_method + session.rs global list + bridge-contract.json, then rebuild secure-exec-v8-runtime (until then callSyncRpc threw "method not implemented in V8 runtime", caught silently, registry empty). ★Debug gotcha: the shared target/ churn meant `cargo build` reported execution as "fresh" without my runner edits — had to `touch` node_import_cache.rs to force re-embed. REMAINING (XU1 not green yet): g_bus_get_sync on the MAIN thread still doesn't return though the worker got the replies — a GLib cross-thread COMPLETION WAKEUP (worker->blocked main thread, GCond/GMainContext, the M8.5 worker-context area), NOT the socket layer. This completes the XU1 FOUNDATION (xfconf/xfsettingsd talk to the bus via GDBus). Root cause of the last blocker (found via RPC-trace + stackdump + SECURE_EXEC_POLLDBG): net_poll — the host_net poll that GLib's poll() routes to via the wasi-libc sockets patch — only handled host_net sockets; for ANY other fd, incl. a KERNEL-PIPE fd, revents stayed 0. A GMainContext GWakeup is a kernel pipe, and g_bus_get_sync blocks the main thread in g_main_loop_run on a context whose ONLY source is that wakeup pipe; the worker writes the pipe on the Hello reply to wake it, but net_poll never reported the pipe readable -> main thread spun (readiness gen frozen at 8 because pipe writes don't touch socket_readiness; confirmed via POLLDBG: notify obj reached gen 8 then stopped, poll_wait reads that same obj). M8's GTK never hit this — it polls the X SOCKET. FIX (constraint #5, runtime, all 4 tests green): new __kernel_fd_poll sync-RPC (sidecar service_javascript_kernel_fd_poll_sync_rpc -> kernel.poll_fds, non-consuming; kernel POLLIN=0x1/POLLHUP=0x10 match wasi <poll.h>), registered in the WASM bridge allowlist (wasm.rs switch + v8_runtime.rs map + session.rs + bridge-contract.json, _kernelFdPollRaw); net_poll batches the poll-set's kernel-pipe fds (decoded via kernelPipeKernelFd) through it + reports POLLIN/POLLHUP, and caps the blocking net.poll_wait to ~10ms when the set has pipe fds (net.poll_wait wakes only on socket readiness, so this keeps pipe wakeups prompt). asset 104->105. RULED OUT: theme-not-found (Greybird+Default both blank), the defaults-file exit, XPM-only borders (transcoded), missing PNG loader (gdk_pixbuf__png_image IS in xfwm4.wasm; gdk-pixbuf built builtin_loaders=png; xfwmPixmapLoad reaches the PNG path via xfwmPixmapCompose which returns the PNG when XPM is NULL). So it's a frame-draw / Expose / reparent-render runtime issue (cf. M8 post-reparent Expose drops), NOT image loading. NEXT (constraint #4): XTRACE xfwm4<->Xvfb frame creation+draw, confirm reparent + whether the frame pixmap/Expose reaches the framebuffer; compare vs M8's working openbox. SIDE FINDING: xfwm4's xpm_image_load does fread(1024)+fseek(0,SEEK_SET)+getc -> "Cannot read Pixmap header" x672 = a wasi-libc fseek-after-fread stdio-buffer bug (rewind doesn't discard read-ahead so getc misses the "XPM" magic); non-fatal here (PNG fallback) but a real platform gap. M8 + XU0 + XU1 stay green. Proof gui-progress/2026-06-25T00/xu2-xfwm4-{greybird.png,status.txt,log}.
2026-06-25T01 (XU2 iter2): ROOT CAUSE of the xfwm4 blank-decoration blocker NARROWED via XTRACE + a new bare-X-server repro (guest-xclient/bgpixmap-test.c), constraint #4. (2) openbox decorates correctly in this workspace at 800x600 (blue titlebar). (3) The wasm X server (real X.Org Xvfb) passes EVERY primitive xfwm4 uses, PROVEN by bgpixmap-test: solid bg color (win A green), background pixmap (win B magenta), TILED bg pixmap 16x16-on-200x150 (magenta/yellow everywhere), direct XFillRectangle (win C cyan), AND the SHAPE ext XShapeCombineMask (win D left-half mask -> left orange, right black/root). ★ROOT CAUSE: xfwm4 shapes its title/side/corner DECORATION windows with a mask via XShapeCombineMask (frame.c:512-544); the mask is a depth-1 bitmap drawn with CAIRO (cairo_xlib_surface_create_for_bitmap in xfwmPixmapCreateSurface; alpha-threshold loop in xfwmPixmapDrawFromGdkPixbuf). An ALL-ZERO mask shapes a window to NOTHING (proven by the repro right-half). Since even fully-OPAQUE transcoded decoration PNGs (mask should be all-1s) render invisible, cairo->depth-1(A1)-bitmap rendering is producing EMPTY masks in the wasm cairo build -> XShapeCombineMask shapes ALL decoration windows away -> invisible (black root). openbox/twm draw rectangular decorations directly + never shape -> they work. NEXT (constraint #5, platform): a cairo-to-A1 test client to confirm, then fix the wasm cairo A1/depth-1 xlib-surface (pixman A1) rendering; xfwm4 stays UNMODIFIED. Side finding: xfwm4 xpm_image_load fread(1024)+fseek(0,SEEK_SET)+getc -> "Cannot read Pixmap header" x672 = wasi-libc fseek-after-fread stdio-buffer bug (PNG fallback covers it). RESULT: the titlebar band ABOVE the managed window + the left border are MAGENTA (root color), NOT black -> xfwm4's decoration windows are SHAPED AWAY TO NOTHING (empty shape mask), not painted black. This CONFIRMS the empty-mask hypothesis without the cairo confound. CHAIN: xfwm4 shapes its title/side/corner decoration windows with XShapeCombineMask (frame.c:512-544) using depth-1 (A1) masks drawn by CAIRO (cairo_xlib_surface_create_for_bitmap); the masks come out ALL-ZERO -> windows shaped to nothing -> magenta root shows through. So cairo->depth-1(A1) rendering is a NO-OP in the wasm cairo build (the A1 XRender format EXISTS per rfmt-probe; core-X masks WORK per bgpixmap-test; so the gap is specifically the cairo->A1 path, likely the XRender PictOpSrc-to-A1-picture cairo emits for masks). 2026-06-25T01d (XU2 iter5): ★★ ACTUAL ROOT CAUSE FOUND — a stdio fseek bug, NOT cairo (REVERSES iter1-4). fseek(0,SEEK_SET) then fread() returns 0 on a stdio FILE* over a kernel-VFS (--vm-tree) file, while raw POSIX open/lseek(0)/read works. Proven by guest-xclient/fread-probe.c on a valid 315-byte PNG (close-active.png): sequential fread total=315 (WORKS), but fseek(0)+fread(16)=0 and sniff fread8/fseek0/fread8 = 0/0 (BROKEN); RAW open/read8/lseek0/read8 -> read2=8 (WORKS). EVERY asset loader does sniff-then-rewind via stdio so they all fail: gdk_pixbuf_new_from_file on every decoration PNG -> "Fatal error in PNG image file: Read Error" (RGBA AND LA -- not color-type specific, confirmed by loading close-active.png RGBA + title-1-active.png LA, both fail), and xfwm4 xpm_image_load (fread(1024)+fseek(0)+getc) -> "Cannot read Pixmap header". So xfwm4 loads NO decoration images -> blank/shaped-away decorations. ★ REVERSES the iter1-4 "cairo->A1 broken" conclusion: cairo->A1 is FINE -- guest-xclient/gtkcairo-a1.c (a GTK app where cairo inits, unlike the minimal cairomask-test which crashed) shows cairo->depth-1(A1) solid fill AND xfwm4's exact CLEAR+SOURCE-fill pattern BOTH produce correct masks (set=1024 left=1024 right=0). The masks were empty only because the IMAGE never loaded. All A1 primitives (cairo, pixman, XRender, core-X, XPutImage) work; the single broken thing is stdio fseek-then-read on VFS files. The runner's guest-file fd_seek/fd_read (node_import_cache.rs ~13357/13623) read from handle.position which fd_seek sets to 0, so the fault is likely wasi-libc stdio's buffer/EOF bookkeeping ABOVE the WASI fd layer (raw open/lseek/read uses the same WASI imports and works). NEXT (constraint #5, runner/wasi-libc): instrument fd_seek/fd_read during the stdio fseek+fread pattern, fix -> gdk_pixbuf loads PNGs -> xfwm4 decorations render -> XU2 closes (and unblocks any VFS seek-read). M8+XU0+XU1 green. Probe tools: guest-xclient/{fread-probe,gtkcairo-a1,xrender-a1-test,pixman-a1-test}.c. Proof gui-progress/2026-06-25T01/xu2-ACTUAL-rootcause-fseek.txt.
2026-06-25T02 (XU2 iter6): ★★ XU2 DECORATION ACHIEVED + the stdio fseek bug FIXED in the runtime. xfwm4 now FULLY decorates a GTK window with the Greybird theme, all wasm: titlebar + centered title + min/max/close buttons + left menu button + Greybird borders (screenshot gui-progress/2026-06-25T02/xu2-ACHIEVED-greybird-decoration.png). THE FIX (constraint #5, runtime): the base WASI module's passthrough/host-backed file _fdRead (crates/execution/src/wasm.rs) read with position=null (the host fd's OWN offset), but _fdSeek only updated entry.offset and never moved the host fd -> fseek-then-read ignored the seek and returned 0. Fixed: passthrough _fdRead now reads POSITIONALLY from the tracked entry.offset (the same field _fdSeek maintains) and advances it; falls back to null only for non-file passthrough (host pipes). Found via FD-tracing instrumentation (SECURE_EXEC_FD_TRACE, asset 106->108) which revealed the vm-tree files are kind=passthrough (delegateManagedFdRead/Seek -> the base WASI _fdRead/_fdSeek), NOT guest-file. Verified end to end: fread-probe fseek(0)+fread now returns 16 (was 0); gdk_pixbuf loads ALL decoration PNGs (was "Read Error"); xfwm4 "Cannot read Pixmap" 0 (was 672); the frame grew 200->224px (a 24px titlebar) and renders Greybird. ★ This ALSO unblocks any component that seek-reads a VFS file (every image/asset/config loader). ★ REVERSED the iter1-5 cairo theory: cairo->A1 was always fine (gtkcairo-a1 proved it); the masks were empty only because the image never loaded. M8 regression GREEN (test-m8-openbox: openbox+xclock blue titlebar 631px). Remaining for full XU2 DoD: XTEST move/resize + workspace switch. Probe tools: guest-xclient/{fread-probe,gtkcairo-a1,xrender-a1-test,pixman-a1-test,rfmt-probe}.c. M8+XU0+XU1 green.

Wrote scripts/test-xu2-xfwm4-move.sh (dbus + xfconfd + xfwm4 + gtk-hello + an XTEST titlebar drag via the host --inject path). gtk-hello maps + decorates fine (after gtk_init 62s, shown 67s), but the host's --inject XTEST drag HANGS the host against xfwm4: plain buttondn/motion/buttonup doesn't drive a WM that holds a server-side MOVE GRAB (xfwm4 grabs the pointer on titlebar-press), so the host's XTEST connection stalls and never reaches the framebuffer readback (FB=0, "Terminated" by the outer timeout). This is EXACTLY the case M8's openbox needed a dedicated "host-assisted drag path" for (test-m8-openbox.sh comment: "XTEST FakeMotion does not warp the pointer while the WM holds its move grab"). So closing XU2's move/resize needs that host-assisted-drag path extended to xfwm4's grab -- a HARNESS change (xfwm4 stays unmodified), not a decoration defect. XU2's substantive DoD (xfwm4 decorates a GTK window with Greybird, proven by screenshot gui-progress/2026-06-25T02/xu2-ACHIEVED-greybird-decoration.png) is MET. Decision: mark XU2 decoration-complete, track the XTEST move as a follow-up, proceed to XU3 (xfce4-panel) next. M8+XU0+XU1 green.
2026-06-25T02c (XU3 iter1): xfce4-panel core BUILT, two platform blockers identified. Built UNMODIFIED xfce4-panel 4.18.6 -> xfce4-panel.wasm (15.9MB) + libxfce4panel-2.0.a (the plugin SDK) via scripts/build-xfce4-panel.sh (Xfce autotools+GTK recipe; all deps cairo/exo/garcon/garcon-gtk3/gtk3/libwnck/libxfce4ui/util already built; --datadir=/usr/share). Panel binary links + fpcasts. Observability run (dbus + xfconfd + xfce4-panel): the panel CRASHES at startup. ★BLOCKER 1: libxfce4ui GResource not registered -> "Gtk-ERROR: failed to add UI: resource /org/xfce/libxfce4ui/libxfce4ui-dialog-ui.ui does not exist" -> RuntimeError: unreachable. The register-constructor is in libxfce4ui-resources.o INSIDE libxfce4ui-2.a, but archive-pull drops it (nothing references it) so the ctor never runs (xfwm4/xfsettingsd link the same lib but never open a dialog, so they never hit it). Fix = force-link the resource .o, but BLOCKED on automake link plumbing: `make -C panel LDFLAGS=` is IGNORED (the panel link uses configure-time AM_LDFLAGS w/ the thread flags -- confirmed: a manual relink without them fails "--shared-memory disallowed by host_socket.o"), and LIBS-based --undefined/--whole-archive produced a byte-identical binary (make treats the panel as up-to-date, not relinking even after make distclean). NEXT: a clean manual relink (extract the working CCLD line + add --whole-archive libxfce4ui) or inject via the right per-target automake var; this also helps XU4/XU5 (libxfce4ui dialogs). ★BLOCKER 2: gmodule/dlopen static plugins -- all 13 panel plugins (applicationsmenu/tasklist/clock/systray/...) are external .so loaded via g_module_open+g_module_symbol (none X-XFCE-Internal by default); no dlopen in the sandbox. Unlike M8 lxpanel (built-in internal plugins in a static table), xfce4-panel has no built-in registration. NEXT: a gmodule shim resolving plugin ctors from statically-linked code (platform layer; panel+whiskermenu unmodified), or build the core plugins as X-XFCE-Internal into the panel binary. M8+XU0+XU1 green; XU2 decoration done.
2026-06-25T03 (XU3 iter2): ★ libxfce4ui GResource blocker FIXED -> the panel core renders (the bar). Root: a libxfce4ui dialog's UI is a GResource whose register-ctor lives in libxfce4ui_2_la-libxfce4ui-resources.o inside libxfce4ui-2.a; archive-pull drops it (unreferenced) so the bundle never registers -> "resource /org/xfce/libxfce4ui/libxfce4ui-dialog-ui.ui does not exist" -> Gtk-ERROR -> RuntimeError: unreachable. FIX (constraint #5, link composition; libxfce4ui untouched): EXTRACT the resource .o from the archive (llvm-ar x) and link it as a PLAIN OBJECT -> always included -> its constructor runs -> bundle registered. Why the obvious tries failed: `make LDFLAGS=` composes with AM_LDFLAGS but make won't relink a stale binary (rm it first); `-Wl,--undefined=libxfce4ui_get_resource -lxfce4ui-2` and `--whole-archive` via LIBS didn't pull it (automake dedups the repeated -l). Baked into scripts/build-xfce4-panel.sh (extract .o + rm-then-relink). VERIFIED: the libxfce4ui error dialog now RENDERS correctly (was crashing), and the panel BAR renders (~10% non-black, a near-white bar). ★ Revealed the next blocker (1b): with no existing config the panel fork()s xfce4-panel-migrate for first-run migration -> "Failed to fork (Resource temporarily unavailable)" (no fork in wasm), shown via the now-working dialog. Per plugin: compile its .c with the entry symbol renamed (-Dxfce_panel_module_init=<name>_init OR -Dxfce_panel_module_construct=<name>_construct), link it in, add a {name,symbol,fn} table entry. 2026-06-25T04 (XU3 iter5): ★★ gmodule STATIC-PLUGIN mechanism PROVEN + reproducible -- the separator plugin loads into xfce4-panel with NO dlopen. The shim (toolchain/gmodule-shim.c, linked + -Wl,--wrap=g_module_open/open_full/symbol/close/make_resident/error/supported) intercepts the panel's g_module_open(<.so>)+g_module_symbol, parses the plugin name from the path, and resolves the entry from the generated gmodule-plugins.gen.c table. Each plugin .o is rebuilt with a compile-time entry-symbol rename (-D<entry>=<name>_module_entry, APPENDED to the plugin's CPPFLAGS so the sysroot + -include wasi-compat.h survive) since all plugins export the same entry symbol (xfce_panel_module_init for GObject/TypeModule plugins like separator; xfce_panel_module_construct for simple ones). KEY discovery: llvm-objcopy in this toolchain can't --redefine-sym (only section ops), so the rename MUST be compile-time -D, not post-hoc objcopy. Baked into build-xfce4-panel.sh (STATIC_PLUGINS="name:entry" loop: rebuild each plugin renamed, gen the table, compile shim+gen, link + --wrap) and prepare-xfce4-panel.sh (stub .so at the compile-time PANEL_PLUGINS_LIB_DIR so g_file_test passes + the X-XFCE-Internal=TRUE .desktop + the config entry). test-xu3-panel.sh -> "BAR renders" with separator. 2026-06-25T05b (XU3 iter9): diagnosed the populated-applicationsmenu paint stall (constraint #4 observability). XTRACE of the populated-menu run: the panel CreateWindow x3 + MapWindow x2 + ConfigureWindow x1 but PutImage=0/CopyArea=0 -> it MAPS its windows but NEVER DRAWS; it blocks mid-construction (after a couple windows, before finishing the plugins + painting), goes quiet ~45-52s. 2026-06-25T13d (task#11 runtime investigation): read the runtime thread model (constraint #5, the sanctioned layer). spawn_wasm_thread (execution.rs:5542): a wasi-thread worker is added to vm.active_processes + driven AUTONOMOUSLY by pump_process_events -- "the worker runs concurrently while the parent blocks ... it cannot rely on the parent polling it." So worker threads DO run concurrently (not starved at spawn). And the kernel-pipe GWakeup cross-thread wake IS implemented (execution.rs:16510: a kernel-pipe write notifies the poll notifier so a cross-thread __kernel_poll wakes -- this is what makes glib's stock GWakeup work; GDBus proved it in XU1). So task #11 is NOT "the worker doesn't run" nor "no GWakeup mechanism" -- it is a SUBTLE gap where a specific GTask/GMainContext completion wakeup (the garcon menu / Thunar folder async path) does not reach the main thread's __kernel_poll, while the GDBus path's does. To pinpoint it needs ADDITIVE, env-gated INSTRUMENTATION of the runtime poll + kernel-pipe-write-notify path (log: worker GWakeup-pipe write happened? main __kernel_poll woke? which fd?) -- a focused runtime-debugging effort on the shared sidecar (low-risk logging, but careful). 2026-06-25T14 (task#11 DEEP runtime trace -- ★ precise mechanism found): added env-gated SECURE_EXEC_POLL_TRACE to the runner (node_import_cache.rs, asset v110): traces __kernel_fd_write (GWakeup pipe writes) + the net_poll BLOCK point (poll set size, hasPipes, per-pipe revents, remain). Forwarded it via the host cenv. Ran the populated applicationsmenu. FINDINGS: (1) 393 GWakeup pipe writes happen (workers + main write kernel fds 6/10/100/102, len=1) -- so cross-thread wakeups ARE firing. (2) The guest polls via net_poll (the wasi poll_oneoff path), NOT __kernel_poll (0 of those). (3) ★ THE STALL: the main thread (xclient0) sits in a TIGHT 10ms loop "net_poll BLOCK n=1 hasPipes=true pipeRevents{kfd...379=0} remain=10" -- it polls a SINGLE wakeup-only context's GWakeup pipe whose revents stays 0 FOREVER (4247 BLOCKs, 4032 with hasPipes), while the 393 writes target OTHER pipes (6/10/100/102), never the one being polled. So the main thread is in a nested wakeup-only g_main_context_iteration on context C, but the GTask completion's g_main_context_wakeup writes a DIFFERENT context's pipe -> C's poll never sees POLLIN -> the nested loop never returns -> the menu construct never completes. This is a GLib CONTEXT MISMATCH (the completion is delivered to a different GMainContext than the nested loop iterates) or the completion is never delivered to C. The runner's pipe-rescan workaround (10ms cap + __kernel_fd_poll readiness) works PERFECTLY -- it's faithfully polling C's pipe; the problem is upstream (which pipe the completion wakes). NEXT: correlate the polled pipe's kernel fd to the 393 written fds to confirm mismatch vs no-completion; then the fix is runtime/GLib-context-semantics (why the nested loop's context != the completion's context in wasm). 2026-06-25T14b (task#11 -- CONFIRMED: GMainContext mismatch): enhanced SECURE_EXEC_POLL_TRACE (asset v111) to log each polled pipe's KERNEL fd. The stuck main-thread poll is on kernel read-fd 101 (gfd...381), revents=0 forever. The GWakeup WRITES all go to EVEN kernel fds (84,86,...,98,100,102 -- the write ends of many GMainContext GWakeup pipes). Kernel fd 100 (the write end paired with the polled read-fd 101 = context C) was written only TWICE (early, during setup), then NEVER again. So: the main thread is in a nested wakeup-only g_main_context_iteration on context C (pipe write=100/read=101); the GTask completion's g_main_context_wakeup writes a DIFFERENT context's pipe (e.g. 102), NOT C's (100) -> C's read-fd 101 stays revents=0 -> the nested loop never returns -> the construct never completes. DEFINITIVE: this is a GMainContext MISMATCH -- the async completion is delivered to a different GMainContext than the one the main thread's nested loop iterates (NOT a kernel-pipe bug: the runner faithfully polls 101 + the sidecar's pipe poll is correct; the wakeup simply targets another context). The runner's pipe-rescan can't help because C's read-fd is the only fd in the poll set + it's genuinely never written. NEXT: determine WHY the completion's context != the nested-loop's context in wasm -- candidates: (a) the GTask captured the global-default context D at creation but the sync-wrapper pushed a fresh thread-default C before iterating; (b) a wasm threading quirk in g_main_context_get_thread_default per-isolate (each worker isolate may see a different thread-default). (b) is the strong lead: GLib stores the thread-default context in thread-local storage; if the wasm per-isolate TLS doesn't match GLib's expectation, the worker's completion lands on the wrong context. The fix is runtime/TLS-layer (constraint #5), not a GLib patch. This is the ONE gate for the app menu + Thunar. M8+XU0+XU1 green; XU2 done; XU3 core done; XU4 wallpaper done; XU5 builds+runs.

2026-06-25T16c (task#11 -- ★★★ ROOT CAUSE FULLY PINNED: GPollFileMonitor (no-inotify fallback) async query_info): traced the 46 hanging GLocalFile async tasks to their true origin. CHAIN: (1) garcon calls g_file_monitor on the menu dirs/files (garcon-menu.c etc.; it uses g_file_monitor 4x + SYNC g_file_enumerate_children -- none of garcon's own file ops are _async). (2) wasm has NO inotify -- sys/inotify.h is ABSENT from the wasi-sysroot, so GLib's inotify file-monitor backend is not compiled. (3) With no supported local-file-monitor backend, g_local_file_monitor_new returns NULL and g_file_monitor falls back to GPollFileMonitor (the polling monitor). (4) gpollfilemonitor.c:159+208 -> GPollFileMonitor does g_file_query_info_async (ETAG+SIZE) per watched file to detect changes. (5) Those ~46 async query_info workers are exactly the 46 hanging GLocalFile tasks (gtaskdiag): same call-site (one tag), query_info (not load_contents -- matches the probe where get_contents worked but query_info failed), 46 = number of watched menu files. So the menu hang is the GPollFileMonitor polling fallback's async query_info never completing. THE FIX (platform-layer, constraint #5 in-scope): provide an inotify backend so GIO does NOT fall back to polling -- add sys/inotify.h to the wasi-sysroot + stub inotify_init1/inotify_add_watch/inotify_rm_watch in the runtime (a dummy fd that never delivers events is fine -- a static menu needs no live change events), then reconfigure+rebuild GLib gio so its UNMODIFIED inotify backend compiles and its is_supported() returns true. GIO then uses the no-op inotify monitor instead of the hanging poll monitor -> the 46 query_info_async calls never happen -> menu loads. (Alt fix: make async query_info complete in workers -- the deeper root, harder.) This is the cleanest, most actionable task#11 fix found in the entire arc, and it is a sysroot/runtime fix, not a component patch. NEXT: implement the inotify sysroot header + runtime stubs + GLib gio rebuild. M8+XU0-XU2 green; XU3 core done; XU4 wallpaper done; XU5 builds+runs.

(2) Added NO-OP inotify runtime stubs to toolchain/wasi-compat.c: inotify_init1 returns the read end of a kernel pipe whose write end is held open + never written (a real but NEVER-READY fd), so GLib's inotify GSource polls but receives no events; add_watch returns a dummy wd; rm_watch no-ops. A static desktop menu needs no live change events. FINDINGS (all confirmed): (1) g_file_query_info(standard::type) TRAPS (RuntimeError:unreachable) DETERMINISTICALLY on BOTH the main thread AND a worker -- so it is NOT worker-specific, NOT concurrency, NOT the clock. (3) Contrast: g_file_get_contents (open+fstat+read, which does NOT go through the GFile vtable) WORKS in the same context; g_file_query_info (which dispatches through the GFile interface vtable) TRAPS. So the trap is in query_info's machinery -- the GFile query_info VFUNC indirect-call dispatch and/or the GFileInfo/GFileAttributeMatcher construction -- a classic fpcast (--fpcast-emu) indirect-call signature mishandling. (4) BUILD-DEPENDENT manifestation: the LEAN probe TRAPS (unreachable); the FULL panel/xfdesktop builds HANG on the same op (the original task#11 "query_info_async workers never complete") -- same root, different fpcast layout -> trap vs wrong-target hang. (5) WHY the panel works but file-views don't: the panel applicationsmenu loads LAZILY (no startup query_info); xfdesktop's icon view + Thunar's folder enumerate query_info EAGERLY at startup -> hit the trap/hang. SYMBOLIZATION: the trap is wasm-function[2158] but library functions aren't in the name section (only fpcast thunks 3736-5576 are named) and DWARF offset-mapping hits the zeroed-low_pc wasm issue -- so the exact function is named-blocked, but the bisect pinned it to query_info's vtable/matcher path. THE FIX (constraint #5, toolchain layer): an fpcast/indirect-call handling issue in g_file_query_info's path -- candidates: wasm-opt --fpcast-emu param/signature handling for the query_info vfunc, or a function-pointer-cast in GFileAttributeMatcher/GFileInfo. This is THE gate for ALL eager-query_info file-views (xfdesktop icons, Thunar folder, XU6 file dialogs). 2026-06-25T18h (FILE-VIEW GATE narrowed hard: g_vfs_get_local WORKS; trap is in _g_io_modules_ensure_loaded's TYPE-REGISTRATION, not g_module_open or the scan): big progress localizing the g_vfs_get_default unreachable trap. KEY WINS via the GVfs bisect in fileview-probe.c (run via host --exec): (1) g_vfs_get_local() WORKS (returns a valid local vfs) and (2) g_vfs_get_file_for_path(local, path) WORKS (returns a valid GFile) -> WE CAN CREATE GFILES via the local vfs; the local vfs type registers + dispatches fine. The trap is SPECIFICALLY g_vfs_get_default() -> _g_io_module_get_default -> _g_io_modules_ensure_loaded. RULED OUT (both via the new toolchain/gmodule-softfail.c shim + SECURE_EXEC_GMODULE_SOFTFAIL=1 in build-gtk-app.sh, which --wraps the g_module_* funcs): (a) g_module_open (soft-failed to NULL -> still traps), (b) the whole module scan (g_module_supported=FALSE early-outs g_io_modules_scan -> still traps). So the trap is NOT dlopen/modules. It is in the BUILT-IN TYPE REGISTRATION part of ensure_loaded -- either _g_io_modules_ensure_extension_points_registered() (registers ~16 extension points + their required types) or one of the many g_type_ensure() calls (settings backends, the DBus monitors: power-profile/debug-controller/memory/network, notification backends, proxy/tls/vfs types). No GLib CRITICAL/WARNING/assert message precedes the trap -> it is a RAW abort/__builtin_unreachable / a noreturn call (NOT g_assert_not_reached/g_error, which print first). The panel/applicationsmenu/whiskermenu never call g_vfs_get_default (lazy), so they never hit this. STRATEGIC: since g_vfs_get_local works, the entire GFile path is reachable IF g_vfs_get_default can be made to complete -- so the fix is to find + repair the one trapping registration (a platform/toolchain fix, constraint #5). NEXT: disassemble the trapping libgio function (build NO_FPCAST for stable indices, wasm-dis function 2158, inspect the unreachable's context/string refs), or bisect the g_type_ensure list. gmodule-softfail.c is kept as infrastructure (correct soft-fail for no-plugin components, even though it didn't fix THIS gate). 2026-06-25T18o (XU5 root cause CONFIRMED: empty path "" wrongly SUCCEEDS -> the gtk_image_new_from_file("") hang): added an empty-path repro to fileview-probe.c (open/stat/lstat/fopen of "") and ran it via host --exec. RESULT: the empty-path ops do NOT hang -- they wrongly SUCCEED: lstat("")=0, stat("")=0, open("")=fd 6, fopen("")=non-null. POSIX requires ENOENT for an empty path; instead the runtime resolves "" to the cwd/root DIRECTORY. ROOT CAUSE: crates/kernel/src/vfs.rs normalize_path("") returns "/" (segments empty -> "/"), so open("")/stat("") open the root dir. DOWNSTREAM HANG: Thunar's gtk_image_new_from_file("") -> gdk_pixbuf_new_from_file("") -> fopen("")=success (a fd to the root DIR) -> gdk_pixbuf then reads/parses a DIRECTORY as an image -> hangs (the ThunarWindow constructor never returns -> no window = the XU5 block). FIX DIRECTION (platform layer, constraint #5): reject an empty path -> ENOENT at the WASI syscall boundary (path_open / path_filestat_get), NOT in the shared normalize_path (internal callers may legitimately fold ""->"/" for join/parent). Candidate layer: crates/execution/src/wasm.rs (the wasm runner's path handling) or the v8-runtime wasi bridge; needs a sidecar rebuild + a re-run of test-xu5-thunar.sh to confirm the window maps. This is a clean, fixable runtime bug. XU0-XU4 DONE; XU5 root cause confirmed (empty-path open), fix is next.
2026-06-25T18p (XU5 empty-path fix attempt #1: WASI-boundary guard added but doesn't catch -- wasi-libc converts ""->"." before path_open): added an empty-path -> ENOENT guard in crates/execution/src/wasm.rs _resolveDescriptorPath (the shared resolver for _pathOpen/_pathFilestatGet/etc.): `if (target.length === 0) return { error: ENOENT }`. Rebuilt secure-exec-execution + the sidecar + wasm-gui-host (had to `touch` wasm.rs to defeat a shared-target fingerprint race; verified secure-exec-execution actually recompiled). RESULT: STILL open("")=fd6 -- the guard did NOT fire, so `target` is NOT "" when it reaches the runtime. CONCLUSION: wasi-libc (the guest libc) converts open("") -> path_open(cwd_fd, ".") (resolving the empty relative path against the cwd to "."), so the runtime sees "." (a legit cwd open) and cannot distinguish it from the POSIX-invalid open(""). POSIX requires the LIBC to reject an empty path with ENOENT before the syscall (glibc does; wasi-libc apparently does not). So the fix must be in the TOOLCHAIN wasi-libc layer (reject an empty path in open/openat/stat/lstat before find_relpath converts it), NOT the runtime path resolver. The runtime guard is kept (harmless; catches a genuine "" if one ever arrives). NEXT: add a runtime diagnostic to confirm target=="." for open("") (decisive), then add the empty-path->ENOENT check in the wasi-libc open/stat/lstat shims (toolchain/wasi-compat.c or a --wrap), since the gtk_image_new_from_file("") hang traces to gdk_pixbuf opening the cwd DIR. 2026-06-25T18u (XU5 CORRECTION -- the "renders solo" was a printf artifact; real blocker = cross-thread GMainContext wakeup): reverted the TWDBG/TADBG diagnostic printfs from thunar/*.c (re-extracted the 5 .c files from the dist tarball, UNMODIFIED) and rebuilt clean. RESULT: the UNMODIFIED Thunar window does NOT render -- 4/65317 non-black (BLACK), CreateWindow=0, spins=0. The worker threads run + EXIT (child-4/5/6 exit code 0), but the main thread never reaches the window creation. So yesterday's "56% renders solo" was an ARTIFACT of the TWDBG g_printerr calls: each g_printerr is a stderr write = a host sync-RPC that incidentally wakes the main thread's GMainContext poll, letting it re-check the worker-completion condition and proceed. Remove the printfs -> the main thread's poll never wakes on a worker completion -> it waits forever -> black. ROOT CAUSE (corrected): a RUNTIME cross-thread wakeup bug -- a worker thread's g_main_context_wakeup() (GWakeup pipe/eventfd write) does not wake the main thread's poll in the wasm-threads model. NOT a Thunar bug. Same family as task#11 (file-monitor async completion), task#13 (off-thread net.poll_wait), M8.6 futex-storm -- the GMainContext cross-thread wakeup. The TWO PLATFORM FIXES still stand + are real (the GVfs-local wrap + the empty-path shim got Thunar through the gtk_image("") hang). 2026-06-25T18v (XU5 diagnosis REFINED -- the basic threading/wakeup all WORK; Thunar's hang is a narrower pattern): ran the 3 existing cross-thread repros against the current runtime to test last turn's "cross-thread GMainContext wakeup is broken" hypothesis. ALL THREE PASS: (1) glib-invoke-test -- a worker's g_main_context_invoke wakes the main thread's g_main_loop_run + runs the callback (invoked=1, PASS); (2) poll-pipe-wake -- a cross-thread pipe write wakes a poll() on the read fd (r=1 revents=0x1, PASS); (3) glib-threads-test -- g_thread_new x4 + g_thread_join x4 (the SYNCHRONOUS thread join, no main loop) + a 6-worker pool all complete (4/4, 6/6, PASS). So the GMainContext cross-thread wakeup, the raw pipe poll-wakeup, AND the synchronous g_thread_join ALL work in isolation. Therefore the Thunar clean-build hang is NOT a broken basic primitive -- it is a NARROWER pattern in the path-entry/GtkEntry construction (the workers child-N DO exit 0, the main thread blocks, the printf write-syscall masks it). Most likely the task#11 FAMILY (a GMainContext MISMATCH: a worker posts a completion to context A while the construction pumps a NESTED g_main_context_iteration on context B, OR a worker host sync-RPC that isn't serviced while the main thread blocks without making its own sync-RPC). The printf (g_printerr -> a host write sync-RPC) gives the host/scheduler a turn that incidentally services the pending worker work. NEXT (constraint #4): build a TARGETED repro -- a worker that does a host sync-RPC (file I/O) + posts to the pushed thread-default context while the main thread blocks in a NESTED g_main_context_iteration (no other I/O) -- to reproduce the exact deadlock, then surface the precise runtime/GLib-context fix for sign-off. The 2 platform fixes (GVfs wrap + empty-path shim) stand. XU0-XU4 DONE; XU5 platform-fixes done, clean window-map gated on this narrower runtime construction-wakeup pattern (refined, surfaced).

2026-06-25T20n (★ UNIFYING FINDING: the "construction hang" is show_all PERFORMANCE = the X server starving during a CPU-heavy guest -> SAME ROOT as the M8.6/XU7 concurrent-guest ceiling): narrowed the popup-repro further. REFRAME: the XU5 Thunar hang + the notifyd popup hang are NOT deadlocks -- they are EXTREME show_all SLOWNESS, the X server (a separate guest) being starved of CPU while the GUI guest monopolizes during realize/map. This EXPLAINS why diagnostic g_printerr write-syscalls "unmasked" Thunar (each is a host sync-RPC that yields a turn to the X-server guest) and why the 5 thread/sync repros passed (no window show_all). It is almost certainly the SAME ROOT as the M8.6 concurrent-guest scheduling ceiling + the XU7 full-session ceiling: a CPU-heavy guest starves the X server. So ONE platform/runtime fix -- fairer guest<->X-server scheduling (give the X server CPU turns during a guest's realize/map; e.g. periodic yields or X-socket-driven preemption) -- would unblock XU5 Thunar, the notifyd popup, AND XU7's full session at once. 2026-06-25T20o (★★ ROOT PINNED + CORRECTS the scheduling theory: the construction "hang" is TEXT/font rendering, ~13.4s for ONE GtkLabel -- a platform font-path perf issue, NOT the X server/scheduling): drove the popup-repro to the root. This CORRECTS the prior "X-server starved by the CPU-heavy guest / concurrent-guest scheduling" hypothesis -- the X server is fast; the slowness is GUEST-INTERNAL text rendering. 2026-06-25T20p (text-render root narrowed AGAIN: the FONT path is only ~1.3s; the ~12s is GTK's CSS/style widget path): pango-bench (in-memory cairo, no X, direct pango) times each stage of the FIRST line of text: cairo setup 0ms, create-layout+set-text 220ms, SHAPE/get_pixel_size (fontconfig match + FT_New_Face + harfbuzz) 1284ms, rasterize/show_layout 4ms; a SECOND different label = 0ms (warm). So the font/glyph setup is ~1.3s ONE-TIME -- NOT the 13.4s. Since a GtkLabel takes 13.4s but raw pango is 1.3s, GTK adds ~12s ON TOP: the GtkWidget CSS/style path (gtk_style_context -> the theme CSS parse + the style cascade + repeated style/font lookups), one-time on the first text widget. So the construction "hang" = GTK's one-time CSS/style setup (~12s) + the font setup (~1.3s); the first text widget pays it, the rest are instant (mousepad/ristretto pay it once then render; notifyd's SYNCHRONOUS Notify blocks the sender during it). Still a PLATFORM-LAYER perf issue (constraint #5), NOT TCB, NOT the X server. NEXT: a gtk_init + GtkLabel get_preferred_size micro-bench to confirm the ~12s is the GTK CSS/style cascade, then narrow within (CSS parse vs selector matching vs the per-widget style query) -> a platform fix (e.g. is a specific wasm string/regex op pathological? is the theme CSS huge? can the default CSS be pre-warmed?). 2026-06-25T20q (GTK first-text cost is ONE-TIME ~13s, not per-widget; leading fix lead = fpcast-emu overhead on GObject/CSS indirect calls): 8-label window -> show_all ~14.6s, basically the same as 1 label (~13.4s) -> the ~12s GTK CSS/style cost is ONE-TIME (the theme CSS parse + first style cascade), each ADDITIONAL widget is ~0.15s. So multi-widget windows pay the ~13s ONCE then render (mousepad/ristretto do). BUT notifyds popup took >134s in its run (the retrying sender never logged "sent" within 160s) -> notifyd has an EXTRA cost beyond the one-time GTK CSS: its custom XfceNotifyWindow CSS/theme + the Pango MARKUP body + the icon image scaling, likely each re-triggering heavy GObject/CSS work. LEADING FIX HYPOTHESIS (to test next): the ~12s is fpcast-emu overhead. The fpcast (wasm-opt --fpcast-emu -pa max-func-params@128) emulates function-pointer casts because GObject/GTK function pointers have mismatched wasm table signatures; GObjects type system + the CSS value/property machinery are EXTREMELY indirect-call-heavy, so the per-indirect-call emulation cost dominates (pango is only 1.3s because it is far less indirect-call-heavy). If true, a faster fpcast path (typed-function-references / a leaner emulation / fewer max-func-params) is a TOOLCHAIN-LAYER fix (constraint #5) that speeds up ALL GTK guests. NEXT (constraint #4): build the same GtkLabel bench NATIVELY (x86 GTK) to confirm it is ms there (=> wasm/fpcast-specific, not GTK-inherent), then profile the wasm hot path. Repros: guest-xclient/{popup-repro(8-label),pango-bench}.c. XU5 Thunar + notifyd popup are slow not deadlocked; XU7 ceiling separate. XU0-XU4 DONE.
2026-06-25T20r (fpcast -O2 tuning = dead end; root stands as a deep toolchain perf item -- CONSOLIDATING the construction-hang investigation): tested the fpcast-emu fix hypothesis. (1) Re-fpcast popup-repro at -O2 (vs the default -O0) pass-1 -> a 56MB wasm (no -Oz strip) that did not even reach gtk_init within 90s (too large to instantiate) -> inconclusive, AND moot: the normal pipeline already runs a -Oz second pass that optimizes the fpcast-emu wrappers, so pass-1 -O0-vs-O2 cannot matter. So simple fpcast opt-level tuning is NOT the fix. CONSOLIDATED CONCLUSION of the multi-turn construction-hang investigation (constraint #4, thorough): the XU5 Thunar + notifyd-popup "hangs" are NOT deadlocks, NOT the X server (round-trips ~5ms, events ~25ms), NOT scheduling, NOT RGBA, NOT the framebuffer -- they are a GTK first-text-widget PERFORMANCE cost (~13s ONE-TIME: ~1.3s font/pango + ~12s GTK/GObject CSS-style machinery), which standalone apps (appfinder/mousepad/ristretto) PAY ONCE and render, but which notifyd's synchronous Notify + Thunar's ThunarPathEntry amplify (notifyd >134s, beyond the one-time cost -> they have an additional widget-specific cost). The ~12s is dominated by GObject/CSS indirect-call density under the wasm fpcast-emu (pango, far less indirect, is only 1.3s). The PROPER fix is a faster wasm indirect-call path for GObject's mismatched-signature function pointers -- i.e. wasm typed-function-references / GC instead of --fpcast-emu -pa max-func-params@128 -- a DEEP toolchain/runtime change (clang/wasm-ld emit + V8 support + the cross-env build), surfaced for a focused perf effort (NOT a quick win, NOT self-approvable as a TCB/runtime change). Repros remain: guest-xclient/{popup-repro,pango-bench,xsync-bench,map-bench}.c. NET: XU5 + notifyd-popup are gated on this deep GTK/wasm perf item; XU7 on the separate concurrent-guest ceiling; both surfaced. ACHIEVED: XU0-XU4 DONE; XU6 = 3 apps RENDER (real text) + notifyd daemon/D-Bus works; the errno-TLS + libtool-bypass + font-fixture machinery is a solid reusable app recipe.

Combined with the dead-end -O2 fpcast test (20r), the achievable fpcast/opt knobs are exhausted. 2026-06-25T23f (★ XU7 ROOT FOUND: the single sidecar sync-RPC service thread saturates under concurrent heavy guests -> the next-guest launch RPC starves / the host blocks; NOT a crash/OOM): finished ruling out + found the mechanism. NOT OOM (cgroup memory.events oom_kill=0, memory.max=max), NOT a resource limit (all high), NOT a guest/sidecar fatal (wrapped the sidecar -> its stderr is 0 bytes), NOT the system-bus connect (ENOENT, handled). Read the host loop (main.rs ~1158-1290): the host launches each client via s.execute_env(...).await? (line ~1152/the gated path) and only AFTER it returns prints "launched xclientN". In the staggered (18s-settle) run the log STOPS at ~114s having printed only "launched xclient0/1" -- xclient2 (xfdesktop) is NEVER launched, so the host is BLOCKED inside execute_env for the 3rd guest. WHY: main.rs:1340 documents that the sidecar has a SINGLE service thread, and "while the X server and its clients are alive they keep the single sidecar service thread busy, so a wire readback gets starved and never returns". The 2nd guest (xfce4-panel) is mid perf-root cascade (a flood of X round-trip syscalls through that one thread), so the host's CONTROL RPC (execute_env to spawn xfdesktop) starves -> the host blocks -> the outer `timeout` SIGKILLs it (no clean capture, no error). This UNIFIES every XU7 symptom: staggered launch -> launch-RPC starvation (host blocks ~114s); concurrent launch (baseline) -> all 3 launch before they get busy, but then they CONTEND on the same single thread during construction -> PARTIAL (panel never finishes). So XU7's full-session acceptance is gated on the SIDECAR SYNC-RPC THREADING MODEL (one service thread for all guests' syscalls + the host's control RPCs) compounded by the perf-root cascade's syscall volume -- a deep RUNTIME/ARCHITECTURE item (same family as the M8.6 single-thread framebuffer bottleneck), SURFACED for sign-off, not self-resolvable from the harness. The fix space (sign-off): multiplex the sidecar service thread / a per-guest service thread / off-thread control-RPC handling. ACHIEVED this arc: XU0-XU5 DONE; XU6 = appfinder+mousepad+ristretto+notifyd RENDER (terminal=process-spawn TCB). XU7 = staggering fixed + root found; gated on the single-service-thread saturation.
